Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. 266:  
267:  The `inspect` command produces a brief summary about the running lab components. It is also possible to get a full view on the running containers by adding `--details` flag.
268:  
269:  With this flag inspect command will output every bit of information about the running containers. This is what `docker inspect` command provides.
270:  
271: +#### containers
272: +
273: +By default `inspect` shows one entry per topology node.
274: +
275: +Nodes that consist of several containers (ie. distributed SR-SIM) are represented by a single node that carries the management addresses of the node.
276: +
277: +The local flag `--containers` will list every container of a lab rather than just the logical nodes.
278: +
279: +When the lab is selected with the `--topo` flag, only the containers of the nodes defined in that topology file are listed.
280: +
281:  #### wide
282:  
283:  The local `-w | --wide` flag adds all available columns to the `inspect` output table.
284:  
285:  ### Examples
286: diff --git a/docs/manual/kinds/srl.md b/docs/manual/kinds/srl.md

292:  ### Reboot
293:  
294:  A reboot issued from within the node, for example with `tools platform chassis reboot`, restarts the SR Linux container. The node comes back with its links, saved configuration and management address intact.
295:  
296: -To support that, SR Linux nodes default to the `unless-stopped` [restart policy](../nodes.md#restart-policy), and containerlab keeps the links and the management address of each node in an internal `<node-name>-netns` container. The internal container is only listed by `containerlab inspect --all`, and it is removed together with the node.
297: +To support that, SR Linux nodes default to the `unless-stopped` [restart policy](../nodes.md#restart-policy), and containerlab keeps the links and the management address of each node in an internal `<node-name>-netns` container. The internal container is only listed by `containerlab inspect` with the [`--containers`](../../cmd/inspect/index.md#containers) or `--details` flag, and it is removed together with the node.
298:  
299:  Use `containerlab stop`, `start` and `restart` to manage the lifecycle of SR Linux nodes instead of restarting the containers with the container runtime directly.
300:  
301:  Nodes with a user-defined [`network-mode`](../nodes.md#network-mode) do not use the internal container.

111: func inspectFn(cobraCmd *cobra.Command, o *Options) error {
112: 	if o.Global.TopologyName == "" && o.Global.TopologyFile == "" && !o.Destroy.All {
113: 		return fmt.Errorf(
114: 			"provide either a lab name (--name) or a topology file path (--topo) or the --all flag",
115: 		)
116: 	}
117: 
118: 	// Format validation (only relevant if --details is NOT used)
119: 	if !o.Inspect.Details &&
120: 		o.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatTable &&
121: 		o.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatJSON &&
122: 		o.Inspect.Format != clabconstants.FormatCSV {
123: 		return fmt.Errorf(
124: 			"output format %q is not supported when --details is not used, use "+
125: 				"'table', 'json' or 'csv'",
126: 			o.Inspect.Format,
127: 		)
128: 	}
129: 	// If --details is used, the format is implicitly JSON.
130: 	if o.Inspect.Details {
131: 		o.Inspect.Format = clabconstants.FormatJSON // Force JSON format if details are requested
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	c, err := clabcore.NewContainerLab(o.ToClabOptions()...)
135: 	if err != nil {
136: 		return err
137: 	}
138: 
139: 	containers, err := listContainers(cobraCmd.Context(), c, o)
140: 	if err != nil {
141: 		return err
142: 	}
143: 
144: 	// Handle empty results
145: 	if len(containers) == 0 {
146: 		switch o.Inspect.Format {
147: 		case clabconstants.FormatJSON:
148: 			fmt.Println("{}")
149: 		case clabconstants.FormatCSV:
150: 			fmt.Println(
151: 				"lab_name,labPath,absLabPath,name,container_id,image,kind,state,status," +
152: 					"ipv4_address,ipv6_address,owner",
153: 			)
154: 		default: // Table format
155: 			log.Info("no containers found")
156: 		}
157: 
158: 		return err
159: 	}
160: 
161: 	// Handle --details (always produces grouped JSON output)
162: 	if o.Inspect.Details {
163: 		return printContainerDetailsJSON(containers)
164: 	}
165: 
166: 	// Handle non-details cases (table or grouped JSON summary)
167: 	err = PrintContainerInspect(containers, o)
168: 
169: 	return err
170: }
171: 
172: // listContainers handles listing containers based on different criteria (topology or labels).
173: func listContainers(
174: 	ctx context.Context,
175: 	c *clabcore.CLab,
176: 	o *Options,
177: ) ([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, error) {
178: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
179: 		return c.ListLabRuntimeContainers(ctx, o.Destroy.All)
180: 	}
181: 
182: 	var listOptions []clabcore.ListOption
183: 
184: 	switch {
185: 	case o.Global.TopologyFile != "":
186: 		listOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListLabName(c.Config.Name))
187: 	case o.Global.TopologyName != "":
188: 		listOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListLabName(o.Global.TopologyName))
189: 	default:
190: 		listOptions = append(listOptions, clabcore.WithListclabLabelExists())
191: 	}
192: 
193: 	containers, err := c.ListContainers(ctx, listOptions...)
194: 	if err != nil {
195: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to list containers based on labels: %s", err)
196: 	}
197: 
198: 	if o.Global.TopologyFile != "" {
199: 		containers = slices.DeleteFunc(containers, func(ctr clabruntime.GenericContainer) bool {
200: 			_, isNode := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]]
201: 			_, isComponent := c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName]]
202: 
203: 			return !isNode && !isComponent
204: 		})
205: 
206: 		// containers that containerlab did not create (ie. ext-container) have no lab labels.
207: 		nodeContainers, err := c.ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound(ctx)
208: 		if err != nil {
209: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to list containers based on topology: %s", err)
210: 		}
211: 
212: 		for idx := range nodeContainers {
213: 			if !slices.ContainsFunc(containers, func(ctr clabruntime.GenericContainer) bool {
214: 				return ctr.ID == nodeContainers[idx].ID
215: 			}) {
216: 				containers = append(containers, nodeContainers[idx])
217: 			}
218: 		}
219: 	}
220: 
221: 	// --details always describes every container of a lab.
222: 	if o.Inspect.Containers || o.Inspect.Details {
223: 		return containers, nil
224: 	}
225: 
226: 	return logicalNodeContainers(containers), nil
227: }
228: 
229: // logicalNodeContainers returns a single logical topology node.
230: func logicalNodeContainers(
231: 	containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer,
232: ) []clabruntime.GenericContainer {
233: 	// Node names are unique only within a lab.
234: 	type nodeKey struct{ lab, node string }
235: 
236: 	holders := make(map[nodeKey]*clabruntime.GenericContainer)
237: 	hasCPMA := make(map[nodeKey]bool)
238: 
239: 	for idx := range containers {
240: 		l := containers[idx].Labels
241: 		key := nodeKey{l[clabconstants.Containerlab], l[clabconstants.RootNodeName]}
242: 
243: 		switch {
244: 		case key.node == "":
245: 		case l[clabconstants.InternalNode] == "true":
246: 			holders[key] = &containers[idx]
247: 		case l[clabconstants.NodeName] == key.node+"-a":
248: 			hasCPMA[key] = true
249: 		}
250: 	}
251: 
252: 	result := make([]clabruntime.GenericContainer, 0, len(containers))
253: 
254: 	for idx := range containers {
255: 		ctr := containers[idx]
256: 		name := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]
257: 		key := nodeKey{
258: 			ctr.Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab],
259: 			ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName],
260: 		}
261: 
262: 		// Components are named <root node>-<lowercase slot>.
263: 		isCPM := name == key.node+"-a" || (name == key.node+"-b" && !hasCPMA[key])
264: 		if ctr.Labels[clabconstants.ToolType] != "" ||
265: 			ctr.Labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] == "true" || (key.node != "" && !isCPM) {
266: 			continue
267: 		}
268: 
269: 		if key.node == "" {
270: 			key.node = name
271: 		}
272: 
273: 		if holder := holders[key]; holder != nil {
274: 			ctr.NetworkName = holder.NetworkName
275: 			ctr.NetworkSettings = holder.NetworkSettings
276: 			ctr.Ports = holder.Ports
277: 		}
278: 
279: 		result = append(result, ctr)
280: 	}
281: 
282: 	return result
283: }
284: 
285: func toTableData(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails, o *Options) []tableWriter.Row {
286: 	tabData := make([]tableWriter.Row, 0, len(contDetails))
287: 
288: 	for i := range contDetails {
289: 		d := &contDetails[i]
290: 		tabRow := tableWriter.Row{}
291: 
292: 		if o.Destroy.All {
293: 			tabRow = append(tabRow, d.LabPath, d.LabName)
294: 		}
295: 
296: 		if o.Inspect.Wide {
297: 			tabRow = append(tabRow, d.Owner)
298: 		}
299: 
300: 		// we do not want to print status other than health in the table view
301: 		if !strings.Contains(d.Status, "health") {
302: 			d.Status = ""
303: 		} else {
304: 			d.Status = fmt.Sprintf("(%s)", d.Status)
305: 		}
306: 
307: 		// Common fields
308: 		if o.Inspect.Wide {
309: 			// Print all fields on one line, no newlines
310: 			tabRow = append(tabRow,
311: 				d.Name,
312: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s %s", d.Kind, d.Image),
313: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s %s", d.State, d.Status),
314: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s %s",
315: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),
316: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))
317: 		} else {
318: 			tabRow = append(tabRow,
319: 				d.Name,
320: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s\n%s", d.Kind, d.Image),
321: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s\n%s", d.State, d.Status),
322: 				fmt.Sprintf("%s\n%s",
323: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),
324: 					ipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))
325: 		}
326: 
327: 		tabData = append(tabData, tabRow)
328: 	}
329: 
330: 	return tabData
331: }
332: 
333: // getShortestTopologyPath calculates the relative path to the provided topology file from
334: // the current working directory and returns it if it is shorted than the absolute path p.
335: func getShortestTopologyPath(p string) (string, error) {
336: 	if p == "" {
337: 		return "", nil
338: 	}
339: 	if strings.Contains(p, "://") {
340: 		return p, nil
341: 	}
342: 
343: 	// get topo file path relative of the cwd
344: 	cwd, err := os.Getwd()
345: 	if err != nil {
346: 		return "", err
347: 	}
348: 
349: 	relPath, err := filepath.Rel(cwd, p)
350: 	if err != nil {
351: 		return "", err
352: 	}
353: 
354: 	if len(relPath) < len(p) {
355: 		return relPath, nil
356: 	}
357: 
358: 	return p, nil
359: }
360: 
361: // printContainerDetailsJSON handles the detailed JSON output of containers grouped by lab name.
362: func printContainerDetailsJSON(containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer) error {
363: 	groupedDetails := make(map[string][]clabruntime.GenericContainer)
364: 	// Sort containers first by lab name, then by container name for consistent output
365: 	sort.Slice(containers, func(i, j int) bool {
366: 		labNameI := containers[i].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
367: 		labNameJ := containers[j].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
368: 
369: 		if labNameI == labNameJ {
370: 			// Use the first name if available
371: 			nameI := ""
372: 			if len(containers[i].Names) > 0 {
373: 				nameI = containers[i].Names[0]
374: 			}
375: 
376: 			nameJ := ""
377: 			if len(containers[j].Names) > 0 {
378: 				nameJ = containers[j].Names[0]
379: 			}
380: 
381: 			return nameI < nameJ
382: 		}
383: 
384: 		return labNameI < labNameJ
385: 	})
386: 
387: 	// Group the sorted containers
388: 	for idx := range containers {
389: 		labName := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]
390: 		// Ensure labName exists, default to a placeholder if missing
391: 		// (shouldn't happen with filters)
392: 		if labName == "" {
393: 			labName = "_unknown_lab_"
394: 		}
395: 
396: 		groupedDetails[labName] = append(groupedDetails[labName], containers[idx])
397: 	}
398: 
399: 	b, err := json.MarshalIndent(groupedDetails, "", "  ")
400: 	if err != nil {
401: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal grouped container details: %v", err)
402: 	}
403: 
404: 	fmt.Println(string(b))
405: 
406: 	return nil
407: }

409: func printContainerInspectJSON(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails) error {
410: 	// Group summary results by LabName
411: 	// Use a map where keys are lab names and values are slices of container details
412: 	groupedLabs := make(map[string][]clabtypes.ContainerDetails)
413: 
414: 	for idx := range contDetails {
415: 		labName := contDetails[idx].LabName
416: 		if labName == "" {
417: 			labName = "_unknown_lab_" // Should not happen if filters work correctly
418: 		}
419: 		// Assign the *entire* ContainerDetails struct (including AbsLabPath)
420: 		groupedLabs[labName] = append(groupedLabs[labName], contDetails[idx])
421: 	}
422:

424: 	if err != nil {
425: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal grouped container summary: %v", err)
426: 	}
427: 
428: 	fmt.Println(string(b))

430: 	return nil

433: func printContainerInspectTable(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails, o *Options) {
434: 	// Generate and render table using the summary data (which uses relative LabPath)
435: 	tabData := toTableData(contDetails, o)
436: 	table := tableWriter.NewWriter()
437: 	table.SetOutputMirror(os.Stdout)
438: 	table.SetStyle(tableWriter.StyleRounded)
439: 	table.Style().Format.Header = text.FormatTitle
440: 	table.Style().Format.HeaderAlign = text.AlignCenter
441: 	table.Style().Options.SeparateRows = true
442: 	table.Style().Color = tableWriter.ColorOptions{
443: 		Header: text.Colors{text.Bold},
444: 	}
445: 
446: 	headerBase := tableWriter.Row{"Name", "Kind/Image", "State", "IPv4/6 Address"}
447: 	if o.Inspect.Wide {
448: 		headerBase = slices.Insert(headerBase, 0, "Owner")
449: 	}
450: 
451: 	var header tableWriter.Row
452: 
453: 	var colConfigs []tableWriter.ColumnConfig
454: 
455: 	// Lab-level columns (Topology, Lab Name, Owner) AutoMerge across nodes of the same lab.
456: 	if o.Destroy.All {
457: 		header = append(tableWriter.Row{"Topology", "Lab Name"}, headerBase...)
458: 		colConfigs = append(
459: 			colConfigs,
460: 			tableWriter.ColumnConfig{
461: 				Number:    1,
462: 				AutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,
463: 			},
464: 			tableWriter.ColumnConfig{
465: 				Number:    2, //nolint: mnd
466: 				AutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,
467: 			},
468: 		)
469: 		if o.Inspect.Wide {
470: 			colConfigs = append(colConfigs, tableWriter.ColumnConfig{
471: 				Number:    3, //nolint: mnd
472: 				AutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,
473: 			})
474: 		}
475: 	} else {
476: 		header = headerBase
477: 
478: 		colConfigs = append(colConfigs, tableWriter.ColumnConfig{
479: 			Number:    1,
480: 			AutoMerge: true, VAlign: text.VAlignMiddle,
481: 		})
482: 	}
483: 
484: 	table.AppendHeader(header)
485: 
486: 	if len(colConfigs) > 0 {
487: 		table.SetColumnConfigs(colConfigs)
488: 	}
489: 
490: 	table.AppendRows(tabData)
491: 	table.Render()
492: }

494: func printContainerInspectCSV(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails) {
495: 	csv := "lab_name,labPath,absLabPath,name,container_id,image,kind,state," +
496: 		"status,ipv4_address,ipv6_address,owner\n"

506: 			contDetails[idx].State,
507: 			contDetails[idx].Status,

516: // PrintContainerInspect handles non-details output (table or grouped JSON summary).
517: func PrintContainerInspect(containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer, o *Options) error {
518: 	contDetails := make([]clabtypes.ContainerDetails, 0, len(containers))
519: 
520: 	// Gather summary details of each container
521: 	for idx := range containers {
522: 		absPath := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.TopoFile]
523: 
524: 		shortPath, err := getShortestTopologyPath(absPath)
525: 		if err != nil {
526: 			log.Warnf(
527: 				"failed to get relative topology path for container %s: %v, using raw path %q",
528: 				containers[idx].Names[0],
529: 				err,
530: 				absPath,
531: 			)
532: 
533: 			shortPath = absPath // Use raw path as fallback for display
534: 		}
535: 
536: 		status := parseStatus(containers[idx].Status)
537: 
538: 		cdet := clabtypes.ContainerDetails{
539: 			LabName:     containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab],
540: 			LabPath:     shortPath, // Relative or shortest path for table view
541: 			AbsLabPath:  absPath,   // Absolute path for JSON view
542: 			Image:       containers[idx].Image,
543: 			State:       containers[idx].State,
544: 			Status:      status,
545: 			IPv4Address: containers[idx].GetContainerIPv4(),
546: 			IPv6Address: containers[idx].GetContainerIPv6(),
547: 			ContainerID: containers[idx].ShortID,
548: 			// Only lab runtimes set the workload; it adds namespace, pod, container, node_uid,
549: 			// exposed_ports and load_balancer_address to the JSON output.
550: 			LabRuntimeWorkload: containers[idx].Workload,
551: 		}
552: 
553: 		if len(containers[idx].Names) > 0 {
554: 			cdet.Name = containers[idx].Names[0]
555: 		}
556: 
557: 		if group, ok := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.NodeGroup]; ok {
558: 			cdet.Group = group
559: 		}
560: 
561: 		if kind, ok := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.NodeKind]; ok {
562: 			cdet.Kind = kind
563: 		}
564: 
565: 		if owner, ok := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.Owner]; ok {
566: 			cdet.Owner = owner
567: 		}
568: 
569: 		contDetails = append(contDetails, cdet)
570: 	}
571: 
572: 	// Sort summary details by lab name, then container name
573: 	sort.Slice(contDetails, func(i, j int) bool {
574: 		if contDetails[i].LabName == contDetails[j].LabName {
575: 			return contDetails[i].Name < contDetails[j].Name
576: 		}
577: 
578: 		return contDetails[i].LabName < contDetails[j].LabName
579: 	})
580: 
581: 	switch o.Inspect.Format {
582: 	case clabconstants.FormatJSON:
583: 		err := printContainerInspectJSON(contDetails)
584: 		if err != nil {
585: 			return err
586: 		}
587: 	case "table":
588: 		printContainerInspectTable(contDetails, o)
589: 	case "csv":
590: 		printContainerInspectCSV(contDetails)
591: 	default:
592: 		return fmt.Errorf("internal error: unhandled format %q", o.Inspect.Format)
593: 	}
594: 
595: 	return nil
596: }
597: 
598: // parseStatus extracts a simpler status string, focusing on health states.
599: func parseStatus(status string) string {
600: 	switch {
601: 	case strings.Contains(status, "unhealthy"):
602: 		return "unhealthy"
603: 	case strings.Contains(status, "health: starting"):
604: 		return "health: starting"
605: 	case strings.Contains(status, "healthy"):
606: 		return "healthy"
607: 	default:
608: 		// Return original status if no specific health info found
609: 		return status
610: 	}
611: }
612: 
613: // ipWithoutPrefix removes the CIDR prefix length from an IP address string.
614: // Returns "N/A" if input contains "N/A".
615: // Returns original string if it doesn't contain exactly one "/".
616: func ipWithoutPrefix(ip string) string {
617: 	if strings.Contains(ip, clabconstants.NotApplicable) {
618: 		return ip
619: 	}
620: 
621: 	ipParts := strings.Split(ip, "/")
622: 	if len(ipParts) != 2 { //nolint: mnd
623: 		return ip // Return original if not in expected format "address/prefix"
624: 	}
625: 
626: 	return ipParts[0]
627: }

nodeKey{\n\t\t\tctr.Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab],\n\t\t\tctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName],\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Components are named <root node>-<lowercase slot>.\n\t\tisCPM := name == key.node+\"-a\" || (name == key.node+\"-b\" && !hasCPMA[key])\n\t\tif ctr.Labels[clabconstants.ToolType] != \"\" ||\n\t\t\tctr.Labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] == \"true\" || (key.node != \"\" && !isCPM) {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif key.node == \"\" {\n\t\t\tkey.node = name\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif holder := holders[key]; holder != nil {\n\t\t\tctr.NetworkName = holder.NetworkName\n\t\t\tctr.NetworkSettings = holder.NetworkSettings\n\t\t\tctr.Ports = holder.Ports\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tresult = append(result, ctr)\n\t}\n\n\treturn result\n}\n\nfunc toTableData(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails, o *Options) []tableWriter.Row {\n\ttabData := make([]tableWriter.Row, 0, len(contDetails))\n\n\tfor i := range contDetails {\n\t\td := &contDetails[i]\n\t\ttabRow := tableWriter.Row{}\n\n\t\tif o.Destroy.All {\n\t\t\ttabRow = append(tabRow, d.LabPath, d.LabName)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tif o.Inspect.Wide {\n\t\t\ttabRow = append(tabRow, %s\",\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\ttabRow = append(tabRow,\n\t\t\t\td.Name,\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s\\n%s\", d.Kind, d.Image),\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s\\n%s\", d.State, d.Status),\n\t\t\t\tfmt.Sprintf(\"%s\\n%s\",\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv4Address),\n\t\t\t\t\tipWithoutPrefix(d.IPv6Address)))\n\t\t}\n\n\t\ttabData = append(tabData, tabRow)\n\t}\n\n\treturn p.\nfunc getShortestTopologyPath(p string) (string, error) {\n\tif p == \"\" {\n\t\treturn \"\", nil\n\t}\n\tif strings.Contains(p, \"://\") {\n\t\treturn p, nil\n\t}\n\n\t// get topo file path relative of the cwd\n\tcwd, err := os.Getwd()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", err\n\t}\n\n\trelPath, err := filepath.Rel(cwd, p)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn \"\", err\n\t}\n\n\tif len(relPath) < len(p) {\n\t\treturn relPath, nil\n\t}\n\n\treturn p, nil\n}\n\n// printContainerDetailsJSON handles the detailed JSON output of containers grouped by lab name.\nfunc printContainerDetailsJSON(containers []clabruntime.GenericContainer) error {\n\tgroupedDetails := make(map[string][]clabruntime.GenericContainer)\n\t// Sort containers first by lab name, then by container name for consistent output\n\tsort.Slice(containers, func(i, j int) bool {\n\t\tlabNameI := containers[i].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]\n\t\tlabNameJ := containers[j].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]\n\n\t\tif labNameI == labNameJ {\n\t\t\t// Use the first name if available\n\t\t\tnameI := \"\"\n\t\t\tif len(containers[i].Names) > 0 {\n\t\t\t\tnameI = containers[i].Names[0]\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\tnameJ := \"\"\n\t\t\tif len(containers[j].Names) > 0 {\n\t\t\t\tnameJ = containers[j].Names[0]\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\treturn nameI < nameJ\n\t\t}\n\n\t\treturn labNameI < labNameJ\n\t})\n\n\t// Group the sorted containers\n\tfor idx := range containers {\n\t\tlabName := containers[idx].Labels[clabconstants.Containerlab]\n\t\t// Ensure labName exists, default to a placeholder if missing\n\t\t// (shouldn't happen with filters)\n\t\tif labName == \"\" {\n\t\t\tlabName = \"_unknown_lab_\"\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tgroupedDetails[labName] = append(groupedDetails[labName], containers[idx])\n\t}\n\n\tb, err := json.MarshalIndent(groupedDetails, \"\", \"  \")\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to marshal grouped container details: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\n\tfmt.Println(string(b))\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc printContainerInspectJSON(contDetails []clabtypes.ContainerDetails) error {\n\t// Group summary results by LabName\n\t// Use a map where keys are lab names and values are slices of container details\n\tgroupedLabs := make(map[string][]clabtypes.ContainerDetails)\n\n\tfor idx := range contDetails {\n\t\tlabName := *Options) {\n\t// Generate and render table using the summary data (which uses relative LabPath)\n\ttabData := toTableData(contDetails, o)\n\ttable := {\n\tcsv := \"lab_name,labPath,absLabPath,name,container_id,image,kind,state,\" +\n\t\t\"status,ipv4_address,ipv6_address,owner\\n\"\n\tfor idx := range contDetails {\n\t\tcsv += step-start
{
  "type": "step-start",
  "id": "prt_20e81cd710010jIhCcq4FAXiKh",
  "sessionID": "ses_df17e7b15ffezqjn3qqGTFkXv0",
  "messageID": "msg_20e81c713001sjveOsA47wkJus"
}
Reasoning
Notable behavior changes:

1. Base: internal containers hidden when NOT `--all`. Head: internal containers are hidden unless `--containers`/`--details`. The test suite previously used `--all` to show the holder; now `--containers`/`--details` show it.

2. In the head, when `--topo` is used, components (like `sr14s-a`) are kept even when `--containers` is not set... wait no — `logicalNodeContainers` filters components: with topo, first list by lab name filter, then DeleteFunc keeps only containers that are nodes or components of nodes in topology. Then logicalNodeContainers reduces to logical nodes. The test expects `ins -t topo -f json` to show only `["clab-${lab-name}-sr14s-a"]` — the CPM A logical node, with the netns holder's mgmt IP merged in. Interesting: in the test, the topology inspect shows only sr14s-a (there may be other nodes filtered by the robot test? The lab has SR-14s node presumably with license etc.).

Wait, but "Confirm topology inspect hides namespace holder" expects names == ["clab-${lab-name}-sr14s-a"] only — so the lab apparently has a single node.

Now potential issues:

a) In `logicalNodeContainers`, `holders[key] = &containers[idx]` — taking address of slice element. That's fine since containers isn't mutated during the loop (they take `ctr := containers[idx]` copy in the second loop). OK.

b) The second loop: `ctr := containers[idx]` copies, then modifies ctr's NetworkName etc. and appends. Fine.

c) Filtering logic: `isCPM := name == key.node+"-a" || (name == key.node+"-b" && !hasCPMA[key])`. Components named `<root node>-<lowercase slot>`. What about slot names like `sr-1`? For distributed SR-SIM, node "sr" with components sr-1? Wait the test: `ctr("lab1", "sr-1", "sr", false, "")` — NodeName "sr-1", RootNodeName "sr". Hmm wait, that doesn't match "component named <root>-<slot>" pattern with "-a"/"-b"? Actually in the test, "sr-1" — hmm, but expected output only includes "clab-lab1-sr-a". Wait, is "sr-1" a component? Let me think: components of SR-SIM chassis: "sr-a", "sr-b" (CPM A/B), and maybe linecards "sr-1" ... Actually in the test, "sr-1" is a component (linecard?) and gets filtered out because it isn't a CPM. The expected output includes only sr-a. OK.

But wait — a subtle issue: what if a topology node itself is named e.g. "my-a"? Then NodeName "my-a", RootNodeName... for a regular node, RootNodeName label presumably isn't set. For non-component containers, key.node == "" (RootNodeName absent), so `key.node != "" && !isCPM` is false, so it's kept. Only containers WITH RootNodeName label are treated as components. OK.

But what about a regular node named with RootNodeName? RootNodeName is only set for components. Let me check where RootNodeName label is set in the codebase.

d) Duplicate logic concern: `c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName]]` — checks if the root node name is in the topology nodes map. But components of nodes in the topology — is RootNodeName the node's name? Yes.

e) Potential bug: In listContainers with topology, first list uses `WithListLabName(c.Config.Name)`. Then DeleteFunc removes containers not matching nodes/components. Then it adds nodeContainers from ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound that aren't in the list (ext-containers without labels). But there's a subtle bug: the DeleteFunc uses `c.Nodes[...]` — but the components' NodeName label is e.g. "sr-1" which is NOT in c.Nodes (only root "sr" is). It checks `c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[NodeName]]` (isNode) and `c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[RootNodeName]]` (isComponent). For holder containers (internal), NodeName = "sr-netns"? In the test, the holder is `ctr("lab1", "srl-netns", "srl", true, ...)`. Is "srl-netns" in c.Nodes? Probably not. But RootNodeName = "srl" which is in c.Nodes → isComponent true → kept. OK.

For ext-containers (no labels): isNode false ("" not in Nodes), isComponent false → deleted. Then added back via ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound. OK.

f) Now a real potential issue: dedupe by ID uses `ctr.ID == nodeContainers[idx].ID`. Fine.

g) Bug candidate: in `logicalNodeContainers`, the `holders` merge: `ctr.NetworkName = holder.NetworkName` etc. But the holder is the internal netns container which owns the mgmt IP. For a logical node composed of components (distributed SR-SIM across hosts?), the test expects `clab-lab1-sr-a 10.0.0.3` — i.e., CPM A gets the holder's IP 10.0.0.3. And CPM B is hidden when CPM A exists. But wait — in test lab1, we have sr-1, sr-a, sr-b, sr-netns with root "sr". Expected output: "clab-lab1-sr-a 10.0.0.3". So only CPM A is listed, and it gets the netns holder's IP.

But in lab2 (no CPM A): sr-1, sr-b, sr-netns → output "clab-lab2-sr-b 10.0.0.4". OK.

Hmm — but for a distributed setup, components might be on different hosts, and the holder is per-host... In that case `holders[key]` keeps only the last holder encountered. Potential correctness issue but speculative — check how srsim kind works.

h) Ordering: the result preserves input order. In the test, containers given in order tool, tailscale, linux, srl, srl-netns, sr-1, sr-b, sr-a, sr-netns, lab2 stuff. Expected output order: linux, srl, sr-a, sr-b(lab2), linux(lab2). Yes matches input order. But is the docker list order stable in reality? Output sorted later by name in PrintContainerInspect (sort by LabName, Name). For JSON table output, contDetails sorted. So order is fine.

i) The switch in the first loop: `case key.node == "":` — empty case does nothing. Fine.

j) Edge: a node whose name is like "srl" with a netns holder labeled InternalNode=true and NodeName "srl-netns", RootNodeName "srl". In second loop: for the holder, key.node="srl", name="srl-netns", InternalNode=true → continue (skipped). Good.

For the node "srl" itself: name "srl", key.node "" → isCPM false but key.node=="" so not filtered; key.node set to "srl"; holders[key] where key.node="srl" — holder found (key = {lab1, srl}) → ctr gets holder's NetworkSettings. Good — that's the single-node SRL case where the node container itself doesn't have mgmt IP (the holder owns it).

k) Now: ToolType filter — tool containers (sshx) are skipped always now. Base behavior: tool containers were listed? In base, tool containers have `tool-type` label; base didn't filter them out except internal. Actually wait, did base show tool containers in inspect? Let me check. In base listContainers, only InternalNode filtering. So tool containers (sshx) were shown in base inspect and now hidden unless --containers. Is that intended? The docs say "By default inspect shows one entry per topology node." Tools are arguably not topology nodes. This is a behavior change — check tests/docs mention. The docs change only mentions the `--containers` flag. Hmm, hiding tool containers might be intentional. Let me check how tool containers are labeled and whether other tests rely on inspect showing them. Let me grep tests for sshx inspect.

l) Important potential regression: base behavior — with `--all` (`o.Destroy.All`), internal containers were SHOWN. Head: `--all` no longer shows internal containers; only `--containers` or `--details` do. The docs previously said internal container "is only listed by `containerlab inspect --all`" — updated. So intended.

But wait — `--details` in head: `o.Inspect.Containers || o.Inspect.Details` returns all containers. In base, --details returned containers with internal ones filtered when !--all. So base --details (without --all) hid internal containers; head --details shows them. Behavior change intended per docs update.

m) Now the csv/table empty case: `return err` after empty handling — err is nil. fine.

n) A subtle bug: in head listContainers with topology file: the first `c.ListContainers(ctx, WithListLabName(c.Config.Name))` — c.Config.Name is the lab name derived from topology. But previously, with topo, they used ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound only, which lists containers by node names including ext-containers (which might belong to another lab's naming?). Now they filter by lab name first. Potential issue: `c.Config.Name` — what if topology file fails to parse? Not relevant.

Another potential issue: when `--topo` is given AND the lab is not running (no containers), fine.

o) Real bug candidate: In listContainers head, when `o.Global.TopologyFile != ""`, they now also call `c.ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound(ctx)` — an extra runtime API call for every topo-based inspect. In base it was the only call; now two calls. Minor perf cost, O(n) dedupe loop with ContainsFunc — quadratic in number of containers, but n is small. Not worth flagging unless large. P4 at most; probably skip.

p) The `--containers` flag registered with `BoolVarP` shorthand "" — fine.

q) `o.Inspect.Containers` interacts with `--details`: both show everything. Fine.

r) Check inspectInterfacesFn — does it use listContainers? Let me check; interfaces subcommand uses different listing probably.

s) Another check: `PrintContainerInspect` removed the internal-node skip loop, since listContainers now handles it. But are there other callers of PrintContainerInspect that pass internal containers? Let me grep.

t) Now the biggest correctness question: the component/CPM naming heuristic. "Components are named <root node>-<lowercase slot>." For SR-SIM distributed, node names: let me check the srsim kind code to see how containers are named and labeled. E.g. a node "sr" in a distributed setup generates components like "sr-a", "sr-b", and linecards "sr-1"? Let me look at the srsim runtime code.

Also check: what if a regular (non-component) node is named "x-b" and another node "x" in the same lab? "x-b" has no RootNodeName so key.node=="" → kept as its own node. Fine.

What about a node "srl" whose RootNodeName is set but not internal and is the actual node? Not applicable.

Potential real bug: `holders[key] = &containers[idx]` — if there are multiple holders per lab+root (e.g., distributed SR-SIM across multiple hosts each with a netns holder?), last wins. 664: 		// Component fields fill env keys the user did not set. Node env wins.
665: 		if componentConfig.Env == nil {
666: 			componentConfig.Env = map[string]string{}
667: 		}
668: 		keepUserEnv(n.Cfg.ShortName, componentConfig.Env, componentDerivedEnv(c), n.userEnv)
669: 		// Slot identifies this container. A node-level NOKIA_SROS_SLOT cannot apply to every card.
670: 		componentConfig.Env[envNokiaSrosSlot] = c.Slot
671: 
672: 		// adjust label based env vars
673: 		componentConfig.Env["CLAB_LABEL_"+clabutils.ToEnvKey(clabconstants.NodeName)] = componentConfig.ShortName
674: 		componentConfig.Env["CLAB_LABEL_"+clabutils.ToEnvKey(clabconstants.LongName)] = componentConfig.LongName

684: 		componentConfig.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeLongName] = n.Cfg.LongName
685: 
686: 		// Preserve the topology env. Init must not snapshot the merged component env,
687: 		// or config generation would treat component flags as user overrides.
688: 		srosNode.userEnv = maps.Clone(n.userEnv)
689: 
690: 		// init the component
691: 		err = componentNode.Init(componentConfig)
692: 		if err != nil {
693: 			return err
694: 		}
695: 		// set the runtime by copying it from the general node
696: 		componentNode.WithRuntime(n.GetRuntime())
697:

699: 		srosNode.rootComponents = n.kindSpecificCfg().Components
700: 		// store base node name
701: 		srosNode.baseShortName = n.Cfg.ShortName
702: 		srosNode.baseLongName = n.Cfg.LongName
703: 		srosNode.rootCtrName = rootCtrName
704:

706: 		n.componentNodes = append(n.componentNodes, componentNode)

708: 	return nil
709: }

17: // usesNetnsNode reports whether the node runs in the network namespace of a holder container.
18: // Nodes with a user-defined network mode keep the network namespace of their own container.
19: func (n *srl) usesNetnsNode() bool {
20: 	return n.Cfg.NetworkMode == "" && !n.Cfg.IsRootNamespaceBased
21: }
22: 
23: func (n *srl) initNetnsNode() error {
24: 	if !n.usesNetnsNode() {
25: 		return nil
26: 	}
27: 
28: 	n.netnsNode = new(clabnodes.NetnsHolder)
29: 
30: 	return n.netnsNode.Init(n.newNetnsConfig(), clabnodes.WithRuntime(n.GetRuntime()))
31: }
32: 
33: func (n *srl) newNetnsConfig() *clabtypes.NodeConfig {
34: 	labels := maps.Clone(n.Cfg.Labels)
35: 
36: 	if labels == nil {
37: 		labels = map[string]string{}
38: 	}
39: 
40: 	shortName := n.Cfg.ShortName + "-netns"
41: 	labels[clabconstants.NodeName] = shortName
42: 
43: 	longName := n.Cfg.LongName + "-netns"

45: 
46: 	labels[clabconstants.NodeType] = netnsNodeType
47: 	labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] = "true"

91: // containerConfig returns the config of the SR Linux container attached to the holder's
92: // network namespace. The runtime rejects network settings for such containers, as they
93: // belong to the holder.
94: func (n *srl) containerConfig() *clabtypes.NodeConfig {
95: 	cfg := *n.Cfg
96: 	cfg.NetworkMode = "container:" + n.netnsNode.GetShortName()
97: 	cfg.DNS = nil
98: 	cfg.ExtraHosts = nil
99: 	cfg.MacAddress = ""
100: 	cfg.PortBindings = nil
101: 	cfg.PortSet = nil
102: 
103: 	return &cfg
104: }
105: 
106: // linkNetns points the node's netns symlink to the holder, as the namespace path of the
107: // SR Linux container changes whenever the container restarts.
108: func (n *srl) linkNetns(ctx context.Context) error {
109: 	nsPath, err := n.netnsNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
110: 	if err != nil {
111: 		return err
112: 	}
113: 
114: 	return clabutils.LinkContainerNS(nsPath, n.Cfg.LongName)
115: }
116: 
117: // deployedWithoutNetnsNode reports whether the node's container runs without a holder: it keeps
118: // its own network namespace, as a node deployed by a containerlab before the holder does until
119: // it is recreated.
120: func (n *srl) deployedWithoutNetnsNode(ctx context.Context) bool {
121: 	return n.netnsNode.GetContainerStatus(ctx) == clabruntime.NotFound &&
122: 		n.GetContainerStatus(ctx) != clabruntime.NotFound
123: }
124: 
125: // GetNSPath retrieves the Namespace Path.
126: func (n *srl) GetNSPath(ctx context.Context) (string, error) {
127: 	if n.netnsNode == nil || n.deployedWithoutNetnsNode(ctx) {
128: 		return n.DefaultNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
129: 	}
130: 
131: 	return n.netnsNode.GetNSPath(ctx)
132: }
133: 
134: // DeleteNetnsSymlink deletes the symlink file created for the container netns.
135: func (n *srl) DeleteNetnsSymlink() error {
136: 	if n.netnsNode == nil {
137: 		return n.DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink()
138: 	}
139: 
140: 	return errors.Join(n.DefaultNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink(), n.netnsNode.DeleteNetnsSymlink())
141: }

Reasoning
So distributed SR-SIM components are named `<base>-<lowercase slot>` where slots can be arbitrary (e.g., "1", "10", "C", etc.). CPM slots are "a"/"b" (case-insensitive). The inspect heuristic matches: CPM = name == root+"-a" or (root+"-b" && !hasCPMA). But note: slots can be like "1", "2", "10" (linecards). A component named root+"-a"... The heuristic checks `l[NodeName] == key.node+"-a"`. For a slot like "a", name = root+"-a". OK.

Edge case: a distributed SR-SIM whose slots are neither "a" nor "b" — cpmSlot errors, so components always include a or b. Good.

But wait — what about the case where slot is "A" uppercase? calcComponentName lowercases, so names are lowercase. Fine.

Now — for distributed SR-SIM, the base node "sr" itself: does it also create a container? The base node is a "fabric" — is there a container for the base node itself? In `deployFabric`, the netns holder is deployed, and components. The base node itself has no own container (it's the netns holder). So in inspect list, containers are: holder (internal, NodeName "sr-netns", RootNodeName "sr"), and components (NodeName "sr-a" etc., RootNodeName "sr"). The base node "sr" itself has no container. So in logicalNodeContainers, for the base node case: components sr-a, sr-b (or linecards). Output shows sr-a (CPM A) with holder's mgmt IP.

But what about the base node's own labels? c.Nodes includes "sr" as a node. The DeleteFunc keeps components because RootNodeName "sr" is in c.Nodes. Good.

Now, key question: does `holders[key]` handle multiple holders? For distributed SR-SIM across hosts — the netns holder is a single container in the lab (NetnsHolder is one node). Actually wait, for distributed (multi-host) deployments... `n.netnsNode` is a single node; components join "container:<holder>". If distributed across hosts, components on other hosts can't join a container on another host. Let me not go down this rabbit hole; the sros distributed mode may be single-host only. Actually "distributed SR-SIM" in clab means the chassis is composed of multiple containers on the same host. Fine.

Another edge: two labs with the same name? key includes lab. Fine.

Edge: The tool container filter. Base showed tool containers (sshx etc.) in inspect output; head hides them unless --containers. Let me check tests that rely on seeing sshx in inspect. Grep tests for "ins" and sshx.

Also: another behavior — in head, the topology-based flow lists by lab name first. If topology contains nodes that were deployed by a DIFFERENT lab with the same name... fine.

Potential issue: the first-pass DeleteFunc with topo. When `--topo` is used, listOptions = WithListLabName(c.Config.Name). c.Config.Name — the lab name from the topology. But what if the topology was deployed under a different name (via `--name` override)? o.Global.TopologyName? Let me check how c.Config.Name is computed in NewContainerLab — probably from topo file or name override. Let me check whether Global.TopologyName is set from --name flag and Config.Name incorporates it.

Also potential bug: when using `--topo` with an ext-container whose name doesn't have the lab label — ListContainers with lab-name filter won't return it; then ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound adds it back. OK.

But here's a subtle regression: previously with --topo, internal holders were included in the list and filtered only if !--all. In the robot test "Confirm topology inspect hides namespace holder", the expected output is ["clab-...-sr14s-a"] — meaning now with topo inspect, the holder is hidden and only the CPM A shows with the holder's mgmt IP merged. Base behavior with -t: ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound returns what? Let me check that function — does it include internal nodes? The base test expected only sr14s-a with -t too (the test is unchanged in that part). Wait, base test: "Confirm topology inspect hides namespace holder" — unchanged. In base, `ins -t` calls ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound which lists containers for nodes in topology. Does it include the netns holder? The netns holder is a separate node in the inventory (isInternalNode). Let me check ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound.

116: 	default:
117: 		log.Warnf("Container %v has no namespace set, skipping!", containerInterfaces.ContainerName)
118: 		containerInterfaces.Interfaces = make([]*clabtypes.ContainerInterfaceDetails, 0)
119:

So in the topo path, `ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound` returns the base node's container = CPM A container (name "clab-lab-sr-a"!). Wait — in the robot test, "Confirm topology inspect hides namespace holder" expects only `["clab-${lab-name}-sr14s-a"]`. That single entry likely comes from `ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound` (the base node "sr14s" GetContainers returns CPM A container) OR from the label-listed containers filtered by the DeleteFunc. Hmm wait, the DeleteFunc keeps containers whose NodeName is in c.Nodes (sr14s → but the container's NodeName label for the CPM A container is "sr14s-a", not in c.Nodes) and whose RootNodeName is in c.Nodes ("sr14s" → yes, kept). So label-listed containers include sr14s-a, sr14s-b (if present), sr14s-netns holder, linecards. Then ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound adds the base node container — which is the CPM A container (same ID, deduped).

Then logicalNodeContainers: for sr14s-a (RootNodeName "sr14s"), hasCPMA → yes. isCPM true → kept, merged with holder's network settings. But note: sros.GetContainers already set NetworkSettings to distNodeMgmtIPs... For the CPM A container from label-listed path, its NetworkSettings would be its own container IP? Components join the holder's netns via container: network mode, so they have no mgmt IP themselves (test says "component doesn't have any IPv4 address" → N/A). The holder owns the mgmt IP. So logicalNodeContainers takes the holder's network settings — correct.

Now, the interesting part: for a topo inspect, does the dedupe between label-listed CPM A and node-listed CPM A matter? They have the same ID, dedupe keeps the first (label-listed). OK.

Now what about the sros GetContainers path when cnts[0] exists: it sets cnts[0].NetworkSettings to distNodeMgmtIPs — but in logicalNodeContainers, if the holder is present, the holder's settings override. Both should give the same mgmt IP. Fine.

Now, the standalone SRL case (single node with netns holder): c.Nodes has "srl" node; GetContainers returns node container with holder's network settings merged (srl.go 483-485). In label-listed flow (--all or --name): containers include node container (NodeName "srl", no RootNodeName) and holder (InternalNode=true, RootNodeName "srl"). logicalNodeContainers: node container key.node = "" → kept with name as key; holder lookup with key {lab, "srl"} → found → but the node container's settings were already merged by GetContainers... wait, in the label-listed flow (ListContainers by label), we don't go through node GetContainers; the containers come straight from the runtime. So the node container has no mgmt IP (it shares the holder's netns; docker reports container: mode containers as having no network). The holder has the mgmt IP. So merging holder settings is needed and correct.

Hmm, but wait — for the node container in the label-listed flow, does docker report IPv4 for a container-mode container? No. So merging is needed. Good.

1. `holders[key] = &containers[idx]` — pointers into the slice; fine.

Reasoning
OK. Ext-containers are registered as nodes in c.Nodes (via topo parsing when --topo given — nodes are created in NewContainerLab). So in DeleteFunc, ext-container's NodeName label — wait, does the runtime-listed ext-container have `clab-node-name` label? The ext-container is an external container; the user attaches it via `ext-container` kind referencing an existing container name. Its labels come from whatever the external container has — possibly no clab labels at all. So DeleteFunc removes it, then ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound re-adds. OK.

But note: the DeleteFunc uses `c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[NodeName]]`. If a user has an unrelated container in the same lab name filter with a NodeName label equal to some node name — fine.

Now, edge case: `--topo` where the lab was deployed with a different `--name` override. c.Config.Name is derived from the topo + name flag, so it matches the deployed lab name. OK.

Another edge: with `--topo` and `-a/--all`? Both flags: inspectFn requires one of --name/--topo/--all. If both --topo and --all are given, o.Global.TopologyFile != "" wins in listContainers, so it only lists that lab's containers — same as base (base: topo wins too). OK.

Now let me think about the important check: the `--containers` flag and `o.Inspect.Containers` default binding. `c.Flags().BoolVarP(&o.Inspect.Containers, "containers", "", o.Inspect.Containers, ...)`. Options loaded from... o.Inspect.Containers defaults false. OK.

Now, one concern: `inspectFn` empty-result handling: `return err` — err is nil from listContainers. Pre-existing. Not part of diff.

Now the test file: it uses `ctr(...)` helper. Look at expectations. In the test, tool container: `ctr("lab1", "", "", false, "10.0.0.9")` with ToolType sshx. logicalNodeContainers must skip it (ToolType != ""). Good.

But hmm — `ctr("lab1", "srl", "", false, "")` then `ctr("lab1", "srl-netns", "srl", true, "10.0.0.2")`. Expected output: "clab-lab1-srl 10.0.0.2". Good.

Now let me examine a subtle issue: in the first loop, `case l[NodeName] == key.node+"-a": hasCPMA[key] = true`. But this only registers CPM A existence for containers WITH RootNodeName. Fine.

Now, second loop filter: `(key.node != "" && !isCPM)` — for component containers that are linecards (sr-1), skipped. For CPM B when CPM A exists, skipped. For CPM B when no CPM A, kept. Matches "CPM A, fall back to CPM B" semantics from cpmSlot.

Edge: What if a component is named root+"-a" but it's a linecard slot "a"? Slots "a"/"b" are CPMs by definition (SlotIsInteger check). OK.

Now — a real edge case issue: the holder lookup in the second loop for a component (CPM): key = {lab, root}. holders[key] was set from the internal container. The CPM container gets holder's NetworkName/NetworkSettings/Ports. Good.

But for the standalone SRL case: node container has key.node == "" initially; then key.node = name; holders[{lab, name}] — the holder's RootNodeName is the node short name. Good.

But wait — there's a subtle collision: the standalone SRL node "srl" with holder. In the second loop, the holder itself is skipped (InternalNode). But what if the holder exists but the node container does NOT (node stopped, holder remains — "When only the holder exists, it is returned so that it is not orphaned on destroy" per srl.GetContainers)? In the label-listed flow, the container list only contains the holder (InternalNode). logicalNodeContainers: holder skipped in second loop → result empty → "no containers found". In base: holder filtered when !--all → also empty; with --all, holder shown as "clab-x-srl-netns". So behavior similar-ish. With head, `--containers` shows the holder. OK.

Now, is there a case where the holder map lookup misfires: a lab has two nodes "srl" and another node whose RootNodeName... no.

Consider: node named "x" (non-component, no RootNodeName), and ANOTHER node named "y" with a holder RootNodeName "y". Node "x"'s key.node = "" → then set to "x" → holders[{lab,"x"}] → nil. Fine.

BUT: consider a node named "srl" whose container has NO RootNodeName, and there's ALSO a node named... hmm, what about the case where the second loop encounters the base distributed SR-SIM base node? The base node has no container. OK.

Now the more subtle correctness question: `hasCPMA` is keyed by {lab, root}. In the test, lab2 "sr" has no "-a" → sr-b kept. But consider a lab where a node is named "sr" AND another node is a distributed SR-SIM whose base is also "sr"? Node names unique within lab. OK.

Cross-lab: same lab name can't exist twice. OK.

Now, the label-based flow for `--all`: containers from all labs. Key includes lab. Good.

Now — a potentially real bug: `holders`/`hasCPMA` keyed by Containerlab label. For ext-containers re-added via ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound: they have no Containerlab label? Actually they might have some. `l[Containerlab]` empty → key {"" , ""}. For ext-container from ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound, does GenericContainer have Labels with NodeName? The DefaultNode.GetContainers fetches by name and returns labels from the runtime — the external container may have arbitrary labels (could even have clab labels from another lab!). Hmm. But that was base behavior too (base returned node containers directly).

Wait, actually there's something: in the topo path, ext-containers added via ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound pass through logicalNodeContainers. If the ext container has no clab labels: ToolType "" → check `key.node != ""` false → kept; key.node = name → holders nil → appended. Good.

But if the ext container happens to have a `clab-root-node-name` label set (e.g., it's a container created by another lab where it was a component!), then in the topo flow... e.g., user does `clab inspect -t topoA` where topoA includes an ext-container that is actually a component container from lab B with labels RootNodeName=... The DeleteFunc: `c.Nodes[ctr.Labels[NodeName]]` — the label-listed pass wouldn't include it (different lab name filter). Then ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound re-adds it unconditionally. Then logicalNodeContainers: key.node = "someRoot" != "" → isCPM check: name == someRoot+"-a"? If the ext container is a CPM A of another lab, isCPM true → kept with key {lab of ext container... Containerlab label = labB}. Output shows it. Edge case, mostly fine; base behavior showed it too.

Hmm, but worse: an ext-container that is a linecard of another lab's SR-SIM (NodeName "sr-1", RootNodeName "sr", Containerlab "labB") included via -t topoA: logicalNodeContainers → key.node "sr" ≠ "", isCPM false → skipped! So the ext-container disappears from `ins -t topoA` output even though it's a declared topology node. That's a real (if unusual) correctness edge: `--topo` with an ext-container whose underlying container carries component labels of another lab. Probability low but real. Base showed it. Hmm — is this worth flagging? It's P3-ish. Actually wait, does DeleteFunc also drop it? DeleteFunc happens on the label-listed set only, before appending nodeContainers. The ext-container gets appended after. So yes it reaches logicalNodeContainers and gets dropped if it has component labels. Also if ext-container has `tool-type` label it'd be dropped too. Also if it has InternalNode label "true" (e.g. a netns holder of another lab as ext-container?) → dropped. Edge case, P3.

Now let's consider the more mainstream regression candidates:

A. `--all` no longer shows internal containers (behavior change, documented + test updated). Intended.

B. Tool containers (sshx, gotty, vxlan, etc.) now hidden by default in inspect. Docs say "By default inspect shows one entry per topology node." Tools are arguably not topology nodes; but are tool containers part of c.Nodes (so topo flow keeps them via DeleteFunc, then logicalNodeContainers drops them)? In the base, `ins -a` showed tool containers. Now hidden unless --containers. Is there any test that greps for sshx in inspect output? Let me check tests using inspect with sshx.

C. The dedupe/merge in topo flow: `ListNodesContainersIgnoreNotFound` is called even when the label-list already returned everything — extra runtime call per inspect invocation. Minor.

D. Potential double-count: in topo flow, the label-list pass lists by lab name; the node-list pass lists containers per node via GetContainers (which for srl merges holder settings into node container). Dedupe by ID keeps the FIRST occurrence — which is the label-listed one (without merged settings). Then logicalNodeContainers merges the holder's settings anyway. So OK. But wait — order: label-listed containers first, then node containers appended. Dedupe keeps the label-listed version (NetworkSettings empty for the shared-netns node container), then logicalNodeContainers merges holder settings. Good.

But consider: the label-listed pass happens with WithListLabName(c.Config.Name) — this requires c.Config.Name to be non-empty. What if topo parsing fails to set Name? Then WithListLabName("") filters... let me check WithListLabName behavior with empty name. Look at core/options_list.go line 134.

<content>
1: ---
2: tags:
3:   - Command
4: ---
5: 
6: # Inspect command
7: 
8: ### Description
9: 
10: The `inspect` command provides the information about the deployed labs.
11: 
12: /// note | Clabernetes runtime
13: With `--runtime c9s`, `inspect` reads Clabernetes `Topology` resources and their related kubernetes objects instead of local Docker containers. `inspect --all` lists c9s topologies across all namespaces, or in the `--namespace` override. The JSON output uses `<namespace>/<lab>/<node>` as `container_id` and adds the `namespace`, `pod`, `container`, `node_uid`, `exposed_ports` and `load_balancer_address` fields. See [Containerlab runtime](../../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#inspect) for details.
14: ///
15: 
16: ### Usage
17: 
18: `containerlab [global-flags] inspect [local-flags]`
19: 
20: ### Flags
21: 
22: #### all
23: 
24: With the local `--all` flag it's possible to list all deployed labs in a single table. The output will also show the relative path to the topology file that was used to spawn this lab.
25: 
26: The lab name and path values will be set for the first node of such lab, to reduce the clutter. Refer to the [examples](#examples) section for more details.
27: 
28: #### topology | name
29: 
30: With the global `--topo | -t` flag a user sets the path to the topology definition file that will be used to spin up a lab.
31: 
32: When the topology path refers to a directory, containerlab will look for a file with `.clab.yml` extension in that directory and use it as a topology definition file.
33: 
34: When the topology file flag is omitted, containerlab will try to find the matching file name by looking at the current working directory.
35: 
36: If more than one file is found for directory-based path or when the flag is omitted entirely, containerlab will fail with an error.
37: 
38: #### format
39: 
40: The local `--format` flag enables different output stylings. By default the table view will be used.
41: 
42: Available format options are:
43: 
44: - `json` - outputs the inspect data in JSON format
45: - `csv` - outputs the inspect data as comma-separated values.
46: 
47: The data is provided in the context of a container that belongs to a containerlab lab. Both output formats will provide you with the following data:
48: 
49: | Field          | Description                                                                                              |
50: |----------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
51: | `lab_name`     | The name of the lab.                                                                                     |
52: | `labPath`      | The relative path to the lab topology file.                                                              |
53: | `absLabPath`   | The absolute path to the lab topology file.                                                              |
54: | `name`         | The name of the container.                                                                               |
55: | `container_id` | The unique ID of the container.                                                                          |
56: | `image`        | The container's [image](../../manual/nodes.md#image).                                                    |
57: | `kind`         | The container's [kind](../../manual/nodes.md#kind).                                                      |
58: | `state`        | The container's state. ie. `created`, `restarting`, `running`, `removing`, `paused`, `exited`, or `dead` |
59: | `status`       | The health status of the container. ie `starting`, `unhealthy`, `up` or `exited`.                        |
60: | `ipv4_address` | The [IPv4 management address](../../manual/nodes.md#mgmt-ipv4) of the container.                            |
61: | `ipv6_address` | The [IPv6 management address](../../manual/nodes.md#mgmt-ipv6) of the container.                            |
62: | `owner`        | The [owner](../deploy.md#owner) of the lab.                                                                 |
63: 
64: #### details
65: 
66: The `inspect` command produces a brief summary about the running lab components. It is also possible to get a full view on the running containers by adding `--details` flag.
67: 
68: With this flag inspect command will output every bit of information about the running containers. This is what `docker inspect` command provides.
69: 
70: #### containers
71: 
72: By default `inspect` shows one entry per topology node.
73: 
74: Nodes that consist of several containers (ie. distributed SR-SIM) are represented by a single node that carries the management addresses of the node.
75: 
76: The local flag `--containers` will list every container of a lab rather than just the logical nodes.
77: 
78: When the lab is selected with the `--topo` flag, only the containers of the nodes defined in that topology file are listed.
79: 
80: #### wide
81: 
82: The local `-w | --wide` flag adds all available columns to the `inspect` output table.
83: 
84: ### Examples
85: 
86: #### List all running labs on the host
87: 
88: ```bash
89: ❯ containerlab inspect --all
90: +---+------------+----------+-----------------+--------------+--------------------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
91: | # | Topo Path  | Lab Name |      Name       | Container ID |       Image        | Kind | Group |  State  |  IPv4 Address  |     IPv6 Address     |
92: +---+------------+----------+-----------------+--------------+--------------------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
93: | 1 | newlab.yml | newlab   | clab-newlab-n1  | 3c8262034088 | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.4/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::4/80 |
94: | 2 |            |          | clab-newlab-n2  | 79c562b71997 | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.5/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::5/80 |
95: | 3 | srl02.yml  | srl01    | clab-srl01-srl  | 13c9e7543771 | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.2/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::2/80 |
96: | 4 |            |          | clab-srl01-srl2 | 8cfca93b7b6f | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.3/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::3/80 |
97: +---+------------+----------+-----------------+--------------+--------------------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
98: ```
99: 
100: #### Provide information about a specific running lab by its name
101: 
102: Provide information about the running lab named `srlceos01`
103: 
104: ```bash
105: ❯ containerlab inspect --name srlceos01
106: +---+---------------------+--------------+---------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
107: | # |        Name         | Container ID |  Image  | Kind | Group |  State  |  IPv4 Address  |     IPv6 Address     |
108: +---+---------------------+--------------+---------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
109: | 1 | clab-srlceos01-ceos | 90bebb1e2c5f | ceos    | ceos |       | running | 172.20.20.4/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::4/80 |
110: | 2 | clab-srlceos01-srl  | 82e9aa3c7e6b | srlinux | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.3/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::3/80 |
111: +---+---------------------+--------------+---------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
112: ```
113: 
114: #### Provide information about a specific running lab by its topology file
115: 
116: ```bash
117: ❯ clab inspect -t srl02.clab.yml
118: INFO[0000] Parsing & checking topology file: srl02.clab.yml
119: +---+-----------------+--------------+-----------------------+------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
120: | # |      Name       | Container ID |         Image         | Kind |  State  |  IPv4 Address  |     IPv6 Address     |
121: +---+-----------------+--------------+-----------------------+------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
122: | 1 | clab-srl02-srl1 | 7a7c101be7d8 | ghcr.io/nokia/srlinux | srl  | running | 172.20.20.4/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::4/64 |
123: | 2 | clab-srl02-srl2 | 5e3737621753 | ghcr.io/nokia/srlinux | srl  | running | 172.20.20.5/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::5/64 |
124: +---+-----------------+--------------+-----------------------+------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
125: ```
126: 
127: #### Provide owner information of running labs
128: 
129: An owner is a linux user that started the lab. When `sudo` is used, the original user is displayed as the owner.
130: 
131: ```bash
132: clab inspect --all --wide
133: +---+-----------------------------------+----------+-------+-----------------+--------------+-----------------------+---------------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
134: | # |             Topo Path             | Lab Name | Owner |      Name       | Container ID |         Image         |     Kind      |  State  |  IPv4 Address  |     IPv6 Address     |
135: +---+-----------------------------------+----------+-------+-----------------+--------------+-----------------------+---------------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
136: | 1 | lab-examples/srl01/srl01.clab.yml | srl01    | user1 | clab-srl01-srl  | ea86f40b412a | ghcr.io/nokia/srlinux | nokia_srlinux | running | 172.20.20.2/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::2/64 |
137: | 2 | lab-examples/srl02/srl02.clab.yml | srl02    | user2 | clab-srl02-srl1 | ba7e807235b6 | ghcr.io/nokia/srlinux | nokia_srlinux | running | 172.20.20.4/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::4/64 |
138: | 3 |                                   |          |       | clab-srl02-srl2 | 71006155b70a | ghcr.io/nokia/srlinux | nokia_srlinux | running | 172.20.20.3/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::3/64 |
139: +---+-----------------------------------+----------+-------+-----------------+--------------+-----------------------+---------------+---------+----------------+----------------------+
140: ```
141: 
142: #### Provide information about a specific running lab in json format
143: 
144: ```bash
145: ❯ containerlab inspect --name vlan -f json
146: {
147:   "vlan": [
148:     {
149:       "lab_name": "vlan",
150:       "labPath": "../../srlinux-vlan-handling-lab/vlan.clab.yml",
