        "type": "file",
        "path": "/source/head/docs/cmd/inspect/index.md",
        "text": "---\ntags:\n  - Command\n---\n\n# Inspect command\n\n### Description\n\nThe `inspect` command provides the information about the deployed labs.\n\n/// note | Clabernetes runtime\nWith `--runtime c9s`, `inspect` reads Clabernetes `Topology` resources and their related kubernetes objects instead of local Docker containers. `inspect --all` lists c9s topologies across all namespaces, or in the `--namespace` override. The JSON output uses `<namespace>/<lab>/<node>` as `container_id` and adds the `namespace`, `pod`, `container`, `node_uid`, `exposed_ports` and `load_balancer_address` fields. See [Containerlab runtime](../../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md#inspect) for details.\n///\n\n### Usage\n\n`containerlab [global-flags] inspect [local-flags]`\n\n### Flags\n\n#### all\n\nWith the local `--all` flag it's possible to list all deployed labs in a single table. The output will also show the relative path to the topology file that was used to spawn this lab.\n\nThe lab name and path values will be set for the first node of such lab, to reduce the clutter. Refer to the [examples](#examples) section for more details.\n\n#### topology | name\n\nWith the global `--topo | -t` flag a user sets the path to the topology definition file that will be used to spin up a lab.\n\nWhen the topology path refers to a directory, containerlab will look for a file with `.clab.yml` extension in that directory and use it as a topology definition file.\n\nWhen the topology file flag is omitted, containerlab will try to find the matching file name by looking at the current working directory.\n\nIf more than one file is found for directory-based path or when the flag is omitted entirely, containerlab will fail with an error.\n\n#### format\n\nThe local `--format` flag enables different output stylings. By default the table view will be used.\n\nAvailable format options are:\n\n- `json` - outputs the inspect data in JSON format\n- `csv` - outputs the inspect data as comma-separated values.\n\nThe data is provided in the context of a container that belongs to a containerlab lab. Both output formats will provide you with the following data:\n\n| Field          | Description                                                                                              |\n|----------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `lab_name`     | The name of the lab.                                                                                     |\n| `labPath`      | The relative path to the lab topology file.                                                              |\n| `absLabPath`   | The absolute path to the lab topology file.                                                              |\n| `name`         | The name of the container.                                                                               |\n| `container_id` | The unique ID of the container.                                                                          |\n| `image`        | The container's [image](../../manual/nodes.md#image).                                                    |\n| `kind`         | The container's [kind](../../manual/nodes.md#kind).                                                      |\n| `state`        | The container's state. ie. `created`, `restarting`, `running`, `removing`, `paused`, `exited`, or `dead` |\n| `status`       | The health status of the container. ie `starting`, `unhealthy`, `up` or `exited`.                        |\n| `ipv4_address` | The [IPv4 management address](../../manual/nodes.md#mgmt-ipv4) of the container.                            |\n| `ipv6_address` | The [IPv6 management address](../../manual/nodes.md#mgmt-ipv6) of the container.                            |\n| `owner`        | The [owner](../deploy.md#owner) of the lab.                                                                 |\n\n#### details\n\nThe `inspect` command produces a brief summary about the running lab components. It is also possible to get a full view on the running containers by adding `--details` flag.\n\nWith this flag inspect command will output every bit of information about the running containers. This is what `docker inspect` command provides.\n\n#### containers\n\nBy default `inspect` shows one entry per topology node.\n\nNodes that consist of several containers (ie. distributed SR-SIM) are represented by a single node that carries the management addresses of the node.\n\nThe local flag `--containers` will list every container of a lab rather than just the logical nodes.\n\nWhen the lab is selected with the `--topo` flag, only the containers of the nodes defined in that topology file are listed.\n\n#### wide\n\nThe local `-w | --wide` flag adds all available columns to the `inspect` output table.\n\n### Examples\n\n#### List all running labs on the host\n\n```bash\n❯ containerlab inspect --all\n+---+------------+----------+-----------------+--------------+--------------------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+\n| # | Topo Path  | Lab Name |      Name       | Container ID |       Image        | Kind | Group |  State  |  IPv4 Address  |     IPv6 Address     |\n+---+------------+----------+-----------------+--------------+--------------------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+\n| 1 | newlab.yml | newlab   | clab-newlab-n1  | 3c8262034088 | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.4/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::4/80 |\n| 2 |            |          | clab-newlab-n2  | 79c562b71997 | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.5/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::5/80 |\n| 3 | srl02.yml  | srl01    | clab-srl01-srl  | 13c9e7543771 | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.2/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::2/80 |\n| 4 |            |          | clab-srl01-srl2 | 8cfca93b7b6f | srlinux:20.6.3-145 | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.3/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::3/80 |\n+---+------------+----------+-----------------+--------------+--------------------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+\n```\n\n#### Provide information about a specific running lab by its name\n\nProvide information about the running lab named `srlceos01`\n\n```bash\n❯ containerlab inspect --name srlceos01\n+---+---------------------+--------------+---------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+\n| # |        Name         | Container ID |  Image  | Kind | Group |  State  |  IPv4 Address  |     IPv6 Address     |\n+---+---------------------+--------------+---------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+\n| 1 | clab-srlceos01-ceos | 90bebb1e2c5f | ceos    | ceos |       | running | 172.20.20.4/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::4/80 |\n| 2 | clab-srlceos01-srl  | 82e9aa3c7e6b | srlinux | srl  |       | running | 172.20.20.3/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::3/80 |\n+---+---------------------+--------------+---------+------+-------+---------+----------------+----------------------+\n```\n\n#### Provide information about a specific running lab by its topology file\n\n```bash\n❯ clab inspect -t srl02.clab.yml\nINFO[0000] Parsing & checking topology file: srl02.clab.yml\n+---+-----------------+--------------+-----------------------+------+---------+----------------+----------------------+\n| # |      Name       | Container ID |         Image         | Kind |  State  |  IPv4 Address  |     IPv6 Address     |\n+---+-----------------+--------------+-----------------------+------+---------+----------------+----------------------+\n| 1 | clab-srl02-srl1 | 7a7c101be7d8 | ghcr.io/nokia/srlinux | srl  | running | 172.20.20.4/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::4/64 |\n| 2 | clab-srl02-srl2 | 5e3737621753 | ghcr.io/nokia/srlinux | srl  | running | 172.20.20.5/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::5/64 |\n+---+-----------------+--------------+-----------------------+------+---------+----------------+----------------------+\n```\n\n#### Provide owner information of running labs\n\nAn owner is a linux user that started the lab. When `sudo` is used, the original user is displayed as the owner.\n\n```bash\nclab inspect --all --wide\n+---+-----------------------------------+----------+-------+-----------------+--------------+-----------------------+---------------+---------+----------------+----------------------+\n| # |             Topo Path             | Lab Name | Owner |      Name       | Container ID |         Image         |     Kind      |  State  |  IPv4 Address  |     IPv6 Address     |\n+---+-----------------------------------+----------+-------+-----------------+--------------+-----------------------+---------------+---------+----------------+----------------------+\n| 1 | lab-examples/srl01/srl01.clab.yml | srl01    | user1 | clab-srl01-srl  | ea86f40b412a | ghcr.io/nokia/srlinux | nokia_srlinux | running | 172.20.20.2/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::2/64 |\n| 2 | lab-examples/srl02/srl02.clab.yml | srl02    | user2 | clab-srl02-srl1 | ba7e807235b6 | ghcr.io/nokia/srlinux | nokia_srlinux | running | 172.20.20.4/24 | 3fff:172:20:20::4/64 |\n| 3 | Docs mention the `--containers` section. Note: docs for `--details` don't mention that --details also shows every container. Minor.

Now the docs say the `--containers` docs mention: "Nodes that consist of several containers (ie. distributed SR-SIM) are represented by a single node that carries the management addresses of the node." OK.

Note the docs don't mention that tool containers (sshx etc.) are hidden by default — this is a behavior change from base where tool containers appeared in `inspect` output. Let me check whether any test or doc relied on that. Grep tests for "ins -a" and "sshx".

1: package core
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"context"
5: 	"fmt"
6: 	"sort"
7: 	"strings"
8: 
9: 	clabconstants "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/constants"
10: 	clablabruntime "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/labruntime"
11: 	clabruntime "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/runtime"
12: )
13: 
14: type runtimeNodeGroup struct {
15: 	name               string
16: 	containers         []clabruntime.GenericContainer
17: 	distributed        bool
18: 	external           bool
19: 	rootNamespaceBased bool
20: }
21: 
22: func (c *CLab) runtimeNodeGroups(
23: 	ctx context.Context,
24: ) (map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup, error) {
25: 	containers, err := c.ListContainers(ctx, WithListLabName(c.Config.Name))
26: 	if err != nil {
27: 		return nil, err
28: 	}
29: 
30: 	result := make(map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup)
31: 	var duplicates []string
32: 
33: 	for _, ctr := range containers {
34: 		if ctr.Labels[clabconstants.ToolType] != "" {
35: 			continue
36: 		}
37: 
38: 		nodeName := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]
39: 		if nodeName == "" {
40: 			continue
41: 		}
42: 
43: 		groupName := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.RootNodeName]
44: 		distributed := groupName != ""
45: 		if groupName == "" {
46: 			groupName = nodeName
47: 		}
48: 
49: 		group, exists := result[groupName]
50: 		if !exists {
51: 			group = &runtimeNodeGroup{
52: 				name:        groupName,
53: 				distributed: distributed,
54: 			}
55: 			result[groupName] = group
56: 		}
57: 		group.containers = append(group.containers, ctr)
58: 		group.distributed = group.distributed || distributed
59: 
60: 		if !group.distributed && len(group.containers) > 1 {
61: 			duplicates = append(duplicates, nodeName)
62: 			continue
63: 		}
64: 	}
65: 
66: 	// Pre-existing resources have no containerlab labels, but still participate in apply.
67: 	for nodeName, node := range c.Nodes {
68: 		cfg := node.Config()
69: 		if cfg == nil {
70: 			continue
71: 		}
72: 		if _, exists := result[nodeName]; exists {
73: 			continue
74: 		}
75: 		if cfg.IsRootNamespaceBased {
76: 			result[nodeName] = &runtimeNodeGroup{
77: 				name:               nodeName,
78: 				rootNamespaceBased: true,
79: 			}
80: 			continue
81: 		}
82: 		if !cfg.SkipUniquenessCheck || node.GetContainerStatus(ctx) == clabruntime.NotFound {
83: 			continue
84: 		}
85: 		result[nodeName] = &runtimeNodeGroup{name: nodeName, external: true}
86: 	}
87: 
88: 	if len(duplicates) > 0 {
89: 		sort.Strings(duplicates)
90: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
91: 			"apply supports only single-container nodes, found multiple containers for nodes: %s",
92: 			strings.Join(duplicates, ", "),
93: 		)
94: 	}
95: 
96: 	for _, group := range result {
97: 		sort.Slice(group.containers, func(i, j int) bool {
98: 			return runtimeContainerLess(group.containers[i], group.containers[j])
99: 		})
100: 	}
101: 
102: 	return result, nil
103: }
104: 
105: // NeedsInitialDeploy reports whether the lab has no runtime state yet, i.e. whether
106: // Deploy would perform a fresh deployment instead of reconciling a running lab.
107: func (c *CLab) NeedsInitialDeploy(ctx context.Context) (bool, error) {
108: 	if c.LabRuntime != nil {
109: 		checker, ok := c.LabRuntime.(clablabruntime.LabExistenceChecker)
110: 		if !ok {
111: 			return false, fmt.Errorf(
112: 				"lab runtime %q cannot determine whether the lab already exists",
113: 				c.globalRuntimeName,
114: 			)
115: 		}
116: 		exists, err := checker.LabExists(ctx, clablabruntime.InspectRequest{
117: 			Name:     c.Config.Name,
118: 			Identity: c.labRuntimeIdentity,
119: 		})
120: 
121: 		return !exists, err
122: 	}
123: 
124: 	currentNodes, err := c.runtimeNodeGroups(ctx)
125: 	if err != nil {
126: 		return false, err
127: 	}
128: 
129: 	return c.needsInitialDeploy(currentNodes)
130: }
131: 
132: func (c *CLab) needsInitialDeploy(currentNodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup) (bool, error) {
133: 	if len(currentNodes) == 0 {
134: 		return true, nil
135: 	}
136: 	for _, node := range currentNodes {
137: 		if !node.external && !node.rootNamespaceBased {
138: 			return false, nil
139: 		}
140: 	}
141: 
142: 	state, err := c.LoadState()
143: 	return state == nil, err
144: }
145: 
146: func (c *CLab) setMgmtBridgeFromRuntime(
147: 	currentNodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup,
148: ) error {
149: 	if c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge != "" {
150: 		return nil
151: 	}
152: 
153: 	var bridge string
154: 	for _, runtimeNode := range currentNodes {
155: 		for _, ctr := range runtimeNode.containers {
156: 			// only containers attached to the default management network, or
157: 			// without a runtime network, carry the default network's bridge
158: 			// label. Unknown networks, e.g. ones dropped from the mgmt list,
159: 			// must not contribute their bridge.
160: 			switch ctr.NetworkName {
161: 			case "", c.Config.Mgmt.Network:
162: 			default:
163: 				continue
164: 			}
165: 			ctrBridge := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeMgmtNetBr]
166: 			if ctrBridge == "" {
167: 				continue
168: 			}
169: 			if bridge == "" {
170: 				bridge = ctrBridge
171: 				continue
172: 			}
173: 			if bridge != ctrBridge {
174: 				return fmt.Errorf(
175: 					"runtime lab has conflicting management bridge labels: %q and %q",
176: 					bridge,
177: 					ctrBridge,
178: 				)
179: 			}
180: 		}
181: 	}
182: 
183: 	if bridge != "" {
184: 		c.Config.Mgmt.Bridge = bridge
185: 	}
186: 
187: 	return nil
188: }
189: 
190: func (c *CLab) updateRuntimeInfoForExistingNodes(ctx context.Context) error {
191: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {
192: 		node := c.Nodes[nodeName]
193: 		if node.GetContainerStatus(ctx) == clabruntime.NotFound {
194: 			continue
195: 		}
196: 		if err := node.UpdateConfigWithRuntimeInfo(ctx); err != nil {
197: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed to update runtime info for node %q: %w", nodeName, err)
198: 		}
199: 	}
200: 
201: 	return nil
202: }
203: 
204: func sortedRuntimeNodeGroupNames(nodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup) []string {
205: 	names := make([]string, 0, len(nodes))
206: 	for name := range nodes {
207: 		names = append(names, name)
208: 	}
209: 	sort.Strings(names)
210: 	return names
211: }
212: 
213: func runtimeContainerNodeName(ctr clabruntime.GenericContainer) string {
214: 	if name := ctr.Labels[clabconstants.NodeName]; name != "" {
215: 		return name
216: 	}
217: 	if len(ctr.Names) > 0 {
218: 		return ctr.Names[0]
219: 	}
220: 	return ctr.ID
221: }
222: 
223: func runtimeContainerLess(a, b clabruntime.GenericContainer) bool {
224: 	// internal holders own the network namespace of the other containers in the group,
225: 	// so they sort first to be deleted last.
226: 	aInternal := a.Labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] == "true"
227: 	bInternal := b.Labels[clabconstants.InternalNode] == "true"
228: 	if aInternal != bInternal {
229: 		return aInternal
230: 	}
231: 
232: 	aName := runtimeContainerNodeName(a)
233: 	bName := runtimeContainerNodeName(b)
234: 
235: 	aOrder := runtimeComponentSortOrder(aName)
236: 	bOrder := runtimeComponentSortOrder(bName)
237: 	if aOrder != bOrder {
238: 		return aOrder < bOrder
239: 	}
240: 
241: 	return aName < bName
242: }
243: 
244: func runtimeComponentSortOrder(name string) int {
245: 	idx := strings.LastIndex(name, "-")
246: 	if idx < 0 || idx == len(name)-1 {
247: 		return 0
248: 	}
249: 
250: 	slot := strings.ToUpper(name[idx+1:])
251: 	if len(slot) == 1 && slot[0] >= 'A' && slot[0] <= 'Z' {
252: 		return 100 - int(slot[0])
253: 	}
254: 
255: 	var n int
256: 	for _, r := range slot {
257: 		if r < '0' || r > '9' {
258: 			return 0
259: 		}
260: 		n = n*10 + int(r-'0')
261: 	}
262: 
263: 	return n
264: }

(End of file - total 264 lines)

Reasoning
Important: tool containers have `NodeName` = shortName, e.g. "sshx" — which is NOT in c.Nodes (unless a topology node is literally named "sshx"). So in the topo flow, DeleteFunc removes tool containers (not a node, no RootNodeName) — and they're not re-added. But in the non-topo flow (`--all` / `--name`), tool containers come from the label pass and then logicalNodeContainers drops them (ToolType != ""). So tools are hidden by default now, shown with --containers. That's the intended new behavior presumably.

Edge: a topology node named "sshx" — c.Nodes["sshx"] exists, so in the topo flow the sshx tool container would be kept by DeleteFunc, then dropped by logicalNodeContainers (ToolType != ""). Good.

Now the flags: `--containers` combined with `-t`: with --topo, `--containers` returns all label-listed containers of the lab (filtered to topo nodes/components) + node containers. The docs say "When the lab is selected with the --topo flag, only the containers of the nodes defined in that topology file are listed." Good.

Now let me think about whether there's a real defect in the DeleteFunc for topo mode: it uses `c.Nodes` keys = shortnames. For a node with custom longname/prefix... `ctr.Labels[NodeName]` = shortname. Good.

But WAIT — there's an important case: nodes deployed with a different `--name` than the current one. If a user runs `clab inspect -t topo.yml` where the deployed lab was deployed with the same topo (same name). Fine.

Another important check: `o.Inspect.Containers` used in listContainers before checking `--details`. But what about `inspect interfaces`? It uses its own path. OK.

Now let's check the second loop once more for a subtle bug: for a container that is a component with RootNodeName set and NodeName = root+"-a" (CPM A), isCPM = true. But `hasCPMA[key]` was set in the FIRST loop. The first loop iterates the whole list before the second loop, so ordering doesn't matter. Good.

Now — an actual logic bug candidate: the first loop sets `holders[key]` for ANY internal container with key.node != "", regardless of NodeName. For the sros distributed case, there's one holder. For srl, one holder. But consider a node kind with multiple internal containers per root? e.g., tailscale sidecar is InternalNode=true with... let me check tailscale labels: line 68 in core/tailscale.go sets InternalNode true. Does it set RootNodeName? Let me check.
